ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-07-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00043_d7e7879	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Minister! Mr. Minister! Mr. State Secretary! We have always said that we must ensure the liquidity of companies, and we have guaranteed this through tax subsidies and guarantees. Secondly, we have said that the solvency of companies should be ensured, and that is what we have done with the fixed-cost grant and with the short-time work. Thirdly, we have said that, in addition to the measures for businesses, measures need to be taken for the population, the workers and the workers and many other groups; this is what we have done with the extension of the family bonus, of course by short-time work itself, by means of funds for families at risk of poverty, by means of additional unemployment benefits and many other measures. Fourthly, we have said that we need to stabilise demand, and with this economic package we are doing just that. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Although the gross domestic product, the loss compared with the previous year, is slowly recovering – we are still at minus 8 percent, which is mainly due to weak foreign demand and export – there are almost certain problems domestically, both in terms of consumption and investment. If you are close to the cash desk, you do not buy a new washing machine and then you do not invest in a new production facility. It is precisely these investments, however, which, on the one hand, strengthen domestic demand, on the other, can compensate for the weakness of foreign demand and, on the other hand, ensure the competitiveness of the Austrian economy in the future. This package provides a strong boost: first, private investment is driven by the degressive AF and by the investment premium; secondly, there is direct public investment in climate protection, renewable energy and public transport; and thirdly, demand is strengthened by consumers by reducing income tax and, for example, by increasing the annual budget. With all these measures, it was our central concern that, on the one hand, investments for which incentives are created are turned forward, i.e. that they help to reduce emissions and pollution. Therefore, those who invest in climate-friendly, environmentally friendly technologies have an advantage both in the degressive AF and in the investment premium, and those who invest in fossil investments are disadvantaged by these laws. I am convinced that these measures will be the first step towards a greater transformation in our economy. [[Congratulation with Greens and ÖVP.]] On the other hand, we have also made sure that the measures are socially balanced. In this package alone, there are several measures that demonstrate this clearly: For example, we have - first - limited the amount of loss return to EUR 5 million. Secondly, in the case of income tax reduction, we have ensured that there is also a negative tax element for those who deserve so little that they do not pay income tax, so that they still benefit from this reduction. Thirdly, we have extended the peak tax rate of 55 percent, which is applied with very high incomes, and this, as I believe, is a sign that balance and social balance are important to us. And the increase in the sixth of the year – which was a requirement of the ÖGB – would now be 15 percent decided by this law. I would not let the accusation of the Fleckerlteppich apply, I have mentioned it several times. This crisis in itself has a very special effect on specific areas, and it is, I believe, a sign of the quality of this economic package, that the government is responding to these points and that it is not simply distributing flat-rate money. [[Congratulation with Greens and ÖVP.]] [[Abg. Meil-Risinger: But that's what you're doing!]] If we take over EUR 20 billion in tax money in order to stimulate economic growth, we cannot, as I believe, talk about a crack, especially because we are in a situation in which we are likely to have to take measures over many months to maintain the economy, because foreign demand and exports will remain weak and we will need this support. This package creates a first major step and will lead to a high level of investment and a certain compensation for these weaknesses from abroad. [[Congratulation with Greens and ÖVP.]] I therefore believe that this package will help, and at the same time it will create the first step towards a transformation which, I believe, is highly necessary, even if Mr Fuchs does not like to hear this. Investments in emission-free installations, in environmentally friendly technologies are safely driven by this package of legislation, and in particular because it is relatively large, it will, I believe, also contribute to the start of this transformation. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-07-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00043_d7e12260	Ms Elisabeth Köstinger spoke on a point of view. - Please, Mrs. Federal Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-07-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00043_d7e12285	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. I am very pleased that we are today able to deal with the Forest Fund Act here in the House. Austria's forest farmers have been fighting for years with a massive amount of shade – by storms, by late snow pressure, and especially now, due to climate change, by a massive occurrence of borch beetle calamities. Due to the Covid-19 crisis, the demand for the wood raw material is also massively broken down, especially as regards exports of Austrian wood in traditional markets such as Italy. This has led to a massive fall in the price of wood, and it can be seen that there has also been a massive decline in the decline in the damage. This has worsened in recent months. That is why, as the Federal Government, it is a very important concern for us to invest in the future. This law is the basis for the largest investment package for the Austrian forest we ever had. Together with the Ministry of Finance, the implementing directives will be drawn up in order to allow for rapid application from autumn 2020 onwards. Overall, we have agreed on ten measures that will provide targeted support. I give the honourable Member Hoyos is right, the shady land or the conditions are quite different in the different regions. However, we offer opportunities for all regions to really support them in order to resist and oppose them. One of the most central measures will be the reforestation of damage events. This will mainly serve to ensure sustainable forest functions. Secondly, we focus very strongly on forestry and forest care measures. We will mainly promote vital and climatic forests in order to avoid future damage. This is a very long-term project, however, because the forest does not grow within a few years, but we have to expect decades. We also plan to pay off part of the loss of value. Especially in the Borkenkäfer calamities, we create this for the first time analogous to the Civil Fund Act, also for the infestation by Borkenkäfer. We also support the construction of warehouses, wet and dry wood deposits. It is important that the wood is transported very quickly, especially in the regions where massive beetle infestation is occurring. If it cannot be processed immediately, it must be stored. We are also very quick to remedy the affected forest farmers. We also promote mechanical decontamination measures, because in particular the reproduction of bark-breeding insects it is essential to limit massively. In particular, mechanical decontamination measures will be used in many regions where manual processing is not possible due to the difficult conditions. We will also support that. We promote and support forest fire prevention measures. Because of the drought that has increased in recent years, the forest fire risk is more on the rise. These measures are also intended to reduce the risk of consequences such as erosion, avalanches, floods and rock strikes. However, I am particularly concerned about the research focus on an installation for the production of wood gas and diesel. Ladies and gentlemen, I should like to draw your attention to these aspects in particular. We really have the task of providing for sales markets through the massive damage-wood attack in Austria; this must be especially for forest-farmers. It is also a win-win situation, because on the one hand, we are creating additional markets for the sale of wood, but also regionally in Austria we are fighting the climate crisis because we are becoming increasingly independent of imports of fuels such as diesel and gasoline, and above all fossil gas, which we substitute through renewable gas. There are already plants in Asia, for example, which work perfectly, which have already reached market maturity. This will be one of our central priorities so that in the future, in Austria, we can produce wood gas and, above all, wood diesel, in order to make agriculture and forestry in Austria one of the first areas, as one of the first sectors of the climate-neutral economy and production. This is really a very central and important future project that we are pursuing with this forest fund. The second very important offensive, which we will start with this Forest Fund Act, is an investment in the construction of wood. Austria is a woodland, and in fact we should have already invested much more in the construction of wood. But with this forest fund, we will be able to make real incentives and accents, for example by means of a CO2 bonus. It is important that we invest more and more in wood construction and that we anchor wood as our building material of the future in terms of innovation and the future. We will support the research focus on climate-based forests with practical, applied research projects. However, we will also invest in the biodiversity of the forest – this is also a very important focus that we set. We want to offer our forest farmers in Austria with this forest fund perspective and future. Many have been thinking in recent years about whether it is still profitable to go to the forest, whether it is still retiring at all. - We say yes. We will thus provide massive support. We will also ensure that wood has a price, especially through the additional sales markets. This is a very important concern for us. The forest is our air conditioning in Austria, and at this point I am very grateful to all those who have managed it for decades. This Forest Fund Act provides the basis for making sense in the future. – Thank you very much.

There is really the hope that we can take an essential step towards efficiency and red tape through this research funding law. Especially in these times, we see how important research is. It is essential for solving social challenges, for climate protection, for ecologicalisation, for health, but also for technology; the topic of AI is just an example. On the other hand, of course, it is also a basis for ensuring long-term competitiveness of Austrian companies, ensuring that technology leadership is guaranteed and, of course, wealth and attractive jobs. I may quote Minister Hahn once again, who said: ‘Insecurity is poison for the research site’. Research is “like a shy deer” and “reliability is therefore the best basis for innovation”. – This is precisely the reliability provided by the Research Finance Act. I would like to thank all the Ministries who have been involved, who have finally put them on track, and I hope that the opposition will also be approved. – Thank you very much.

For me, however, the question arises in this context: Where and what is Austria in the European comparison? – The EU Animal Testing Directive and the 2012 Animal Testing Act clearly state that the use of animals in animal testing is to be avoided and reduced as much as possible and the conditions for breeding, sheltering, care and use of animals in animal experiments should be improved. Animal tests must be necessary for the acquisition of knowledge to be approved. Animal testing may only be carried out if no alternatives are possible, and even with the lowest possible number of animals and the lowest possible load. The development of replacement methods, which we all would like to have, is supported nationally and Europeanly. However, a complete replacement of animal tests with validated alternatives is not yet possible. For example, let us look at basic medical research, from which we can all benefit and affect many of us: Killing cancer cells in a cell culture shell is very easy, only it has not been used to invent a cancer drug for a long time. It is necessary to explain whether active substances in patients do not harm other healthy cells or, for example, destroy the immune system. Here I can explain something to my own special area, the black skin cancer, the melanoma: With the help of animal tests, new medicines have been developed in black skin cancer, which are very helpful to patients. If the average survival rate in metastatic melanoma was still at six to nine months ten years ago, almost 50 percent of the population currently survives over five years. Without animal tests, there would be no cardiac pacemaker, no modern surgical methods, no riskless blood transfusion, no Parkinson's treatment, no penicillin, no insulin and no vaccination we are waiting for now. Three-quarters of all Nobel Prizes for Medicine were awarded for progress on the basis of animal experiments. However, some institutes or universities go their own, even stricter way than required by law. At the Linzer Johannes-Kepler University, an animal testing laboratory is planned in cooperation with the young medical community. One goes a new way of offensive communication and discussion on this delicate subject, and that is a good access. The self-created criteria are even more stringent than the legal ones. Instead: Yes, but!, reads here: No, but! Animal testing will have to take a hard look at it in the future before it becomes possible. In this sense, we should all remain sensitive and vigilant on this subject of animal testing. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-07-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00043_d7e15200	Mr President. High house! Valuable Minister! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Dear Seers! The innovation we are deciding on today is not a huge throw, but a necessary adaptation to EU standards. Austria had transposed the EU Directive into national law with the animal testing law in force at the beginning of 2013. According to law, animal testing in Austria may only be carried out if there are no alternatives to it, with the lowest possible number of animals and with minimal load. Animal studies carried out at universities and scientific institutions must be approved by the Ministry of Science, which is advised by an expert committee. The respective authorities of the countries shall be responsible for the approval of all other animal tests. These are essential standards with which we have been driving very well in Austria for years, but as key criticisms of the Austrian regulation, the Commission mentions, among other things, the competences of staff and the rules on project evaluations. In addition to the general reporting obligations to the Commission, the novel is adapted to include legal definitions, ‘self-retaining colony’ and ‘preventing leading clinical status’, as well as the requirements of the personnel involved in animal testing. We are satisfied with the novel, because if an infringement procedure is already under way against Austria, the government must of course act. We're pressing one eye to see that it didn't happen immediately, but only one year later. Basic to animal testing: No one likes them, not our scientists. There is a rather strict animal testing law in Austria in the international comparison, and that is also good. Like all other political groups, we consider the 3-R principle to be essential in order to avoid unnecessary animal suffering to a large extent – replace, reduce and reflect – but, as honest as it is, it is unfortunately not done in research without animal testing. But there are very encouraging and extremely exciting developments in the area of replacement, i.e. the replacement of animal experiments. One of these was accidentally launched in Vienna in 2013 at the Institute of Molecular Biotechnology of Madeline Lancaster. These are so-called organoids: These are essentially mini-organs in Bioreactors from stem cells are bred. Here too, depending on the area of application, it is not entirely without animal cells as starting material, but it is a great step in one direction, animal tests, as good as it goes, to avoid without the quality of research falling. On the contrary: Based on Madeline Lancaster's work, organoids have now arrived in many laboratories around the globe and are used as models of brains, in the exploration of cardiovascular diseases and also of the new coronavirus. They are also extremely useful in cancer research because they can be grown from human cancer cells. Of course, we wish ourselves much more such renewals and have a lot of confidence in science. By the way, this is a very nice example of what happens when you let researchers work in peace and then suddenly and unexpectedly come from a corner a innovation that revolutionizes a whole series of other research areas.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-07-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00043_d7e15468	Thank you. There has been no oral reporting on item 13 of the agenda. Mrs Kuntzl is speaking on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). I can give her the word.
ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-07-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00043_d7e15496	Ladies and gentlemen! We very much hope that the Blümel virus has not arrived at the Bureau, Mr President. We are now dealing with these proposals of new regulations concerning, on the one hand, technical universities, on the other hand, private universities and educational universities. We welcome all the regulations that ensure that quality assurance is improved. Welcome all the rules that ensure greater transparency We do. However, when we look at the critical statements, the many letters we received, calls we have received, talks we have conducted, we note with great concern that concerns, legitimate concerns that exist on the part of the people who work in these areas – that is, qualified persons – are simply ignored, that they are being run over and that very qualified, committed people are being dipped. There you could have had conversations and could certainly change one or the other. I will now deal with the fields of vocational colleges and private universities. In the field of technical colleges, it is to be explicitly welcomed that the law now explicitly mentions the high-school development and financing plan. That's important, that was always a trembling party for the colleges. It's good that this is fixed now. What we lack is that this is also linked to a review procedure and that Parliament is involved. From our point of view, this is a serious mistake in beauty, perhaps even in the next few years. From our point of view, it would also be important to report on the subject of higher education, as would the universities. These are reported to Parliament every three years. This would also be very important for the high school sector in order to deal with this important area of our education system here in the High House accordingly. One point that we expressly reject is the possibility that this law will provide us with the opportunity to buy higher education courses, courses of study. They are to be purchased for defined, demarcated target groups by non-university, private legal entities. This is due to the fact that specialist universities offer tailor-made, closely defined educational opportunities. This could also lead to a financial dependence on these contracting entities. In addition, it is particularly important from our point of view that, if you take a vocational education course, a training course, which is versatile and not just for a particular company, can be used for a particular company. This is linked to the fact that the publication of sources of funding is restricted to public payments and does not include private payments. This is a mixture that really doesn't offer a good picture. For private universities, we very much welcome this point, which creates transparency that the training contracts must now be published on the home page of the private university. What is missing, however, is that the total cost of the study will also be published, which would be an important factor especially for private universities and the high cost of students. One point we see very critical in this area is the introduction of private universities. In future, there will be not only private universities but also private universities, i.e. this law should increase the offer of paid training. In recent years, however, we have seen a trend towards an increase in cost-based offers and an increase in costs. It is also not clear from this draft law which profile these private universities should actually have and who actually needs it. We do not see that there is a real need for that. This simply increases the small parts, the inconsistency and, above all, the commercialisation of training in the tertiary field. What we urgently need is an expansion of high-school courses. That would be an hour's commandment, Minister of Education, if I may address you, not just in the field of science. We know that the current crisis young people In particular, it is particularly difficult to find apprenticeships to find jobs. The command of the hour, Minister of Education, would be to link a package of measures and to provide additional training opportunities for young people throughout the education sector – the secondary schools, the colleges, the universities. That would make more sense than if they were standing on the street in the autumn.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-07-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00043_d7e15580	Mr President. High house! It is true at the technical colleges that these bodies are being strengthened. The colleagues and the head of the College receive tasks of strategic development and also development in teaching. This is, as it were, reflected in the universities. This may not have been quite right for some of those who run the technical colleges, but they have been told that their task is essentially kept nevertheless, their responsibility is also preserved. You can also propose to the head of this body in a three-way proposal. It would therefore not be possible to see why there are conflicts. Yes, it is even possible that this will improve the structure of the University of Applied Sciences. We hope for it anyway. We'll see if it succeeds. We always say: Better is the enemy of good. We can always improve – all this is possible. It is also true that Mrs Kuntzl said that the young people are being offered less. Then I actually don't understand why private universities are not seen as positive. [[Abg. Kuntzl: Because they're expensive!]] This is also an offer where a university is here, where there will not be a doctorate, but the Magisterium is the goal of a fast training, a training more for the present and not for the future. This is also very important and a further building block in the variety of possible offers. The other thing that was still being assembled is that these pedagogical universities are on the ministers' gangband – so it was said in an broadcast. Just the opposite is the case! Now, the educational universities are depoliticized by the fact that the Council of Higher Education is no longer occupied with politicians or with people who are close to political parties, but it is really depoliticized. In a sense, they have become completely free, except for the fact that, of course, they have to train the young people, who will later be hired by the Federal Ministry as teachers. It is quite clear that the goals of the Federal Ministry should be reflected in these educational universities. That is quite natural and not a question. Maybe also a small comment by the way: The word autonomy is always considered as a means of magic, that is, the apotropaion when autonomy is present. There is autonomy as such at Camus in Caligula. There he is autonomous, and the whole thing is absurd theater. True autonomy is always under arrest with context. It is quite clear that there must be autonomy in a certain context, and this one is to negotiate and he is also well negotiated. This will really strengthen pedagogical universities, and they will stand well in an autonomous situation in their implementation of the teaching. On the other hand, they will naturally have to stick to what is set out as a framework. So it's a real autonomy, a contextual autonomy. Finally, I should like to mention a wonderful detail in this amendment. We took it back! We are a baroque country, ladies and gentlemen. We love titles, and the title of the rector – male, female, or whatever gender – may remain in a university. There is a rector magnificus or a Rectrix magnifica, and that is also an essential sign for us that we keep the tradition. For a long time, the colleges live with their rectors! – Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-07-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00043_d7e15630	Mr Kassager has been mentioned on the floor. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-07-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00043_d7e15652	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Federal Minister! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) This item on the agenda is about three essential laws in the field of tertiary education. This is, on the one hand, the Higher Quality Assurance Act with the innovation that now also the pedagogical universities are covered by external quality assurance by the AQ Austria or a recognised quality assurance agency, so that now all universities are covered by the quality assurance regime. We find that good and support it. The second law, which is adopted today, concerns – a long-standing demand of the Freedom Party – that it should be possible in the field of universities to conduct or establish private universities. There's now a private high school law. We see this as less critical, or in that case different from SPÖ, otherwise, as far as private universities are concerned, we have considerable areas of overlap and equal positions. In this case, however, we see the possibility of allowing private initiatives in the field of tertiary education as very positive. I am also on the road relatively much internationally, it is going on and there that the tertiary sector also offers private operators opportunities, which on the one hand will create a healthy positive competitive situation and on the other hand simply increase the offer – that can only be good. But it must not lead – and this is our concern – to create an ore situation in higher education, in higher education, so to speak, that the ministry is now saying: At the same time, we are starved by the public universities and at the same time offer the possibility for the private sector to set up vocational colleges. - That's what we see. In this portfolio of tertiary education providers, the technical universities are anything but the dear children of the Federal Minister; that is my subjective assessment, and this fills us with concern. They are hopelessly underfinanced, but the universities have received very, very nice funding at the time, in the previous GP or in the time of the free play of the forces. We remember: an additional 1.5 billion euros for universities, which is 15 percent more, and the colleges get nothing at all. If, so to speak, the maintenance of the existing funding rates is celebrated as a success, then there is something massively wrong. I should also like to mention that my colleague Dr Martin Graf's request to increase the rate of funding for professional universities by at least 10 percent has been postponed. It is therefore not worth noting that this is set out here in the plenary session of a wider public. We therefore see, on the one hand, a real problem of sub-financing and, on the other hand, no dynamic in this area; that is administration. The colleges are a success story of the last 25 years, they are now celebrating 25 years of their existence, but my impression is – and I don't think I am – that there has been a standstill in recent years, which is very, very bad. We have a huge need, Mr Kuntzl has already mentioned it, and there we are of the same opinion as the SPÖ: to present 1,000 additional places as great expansion is far from what we need. A significant increase in the level of higher education and the places of study would be absolutely appropriate and necessary. A lot has been discussed in the Committee itself, and I do not want to go much further in detail now. There are a few points that we would have made different as a Freedom Party, especially as regards the relationship between management and college. We therefore believe that the potential for conflict from our point of view is to extend the competence of the College in some way. I know very much, very many colleges, have also worked in this field as head of study and was also a member of the technical college conference. Many have called me, and I share their concern that the balance between the College and the ultimately fully responsible and liable directors or the bodies of the corresponding companies is not well solved. - You'll see that. We will be able to agree to the whole because the positive is superior, but we will take a close look at the extent to which our fears then come true in practice or not. It would be a shame if, in addition to sub-financing, an additional brake block would be installed in a very, very dynamic area, in the tertiary area, namely in the higher education sector.

Incite them to maximum benefits through self-responsibilities in research and teaching was my goal, and not to take them to the kandare and actually demise them. That's what's happening now.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-07-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00043_d7e16422	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, Dear Seers! We do not believe that this is really justified and objectively justified, as did all the other parties who have rejected this proposal in committee, in any case in this undifferentiated form in which it is proposed. However, I think this discussion is a very good opportunity to point out a completely alternative model of the existing study contributions, something that we have been demanding for a very long time, namely a model of post-graduate fees, not as a self-evident, but because the starved universities need every cent for more innovation and better infrastructure. According to our calculated model of post-graduate fees, the contributions are not cancelled during the course of study, but only when you are in your professional life and deserved well. This would also greatly exacerbate situations such as the Corona crisis, because the contributions will only become due if you get a decent income. I will briefly explain to you – because this is already so flat-rate rejection – how our model works. We really thought something about it. According to our model, the universities have the opportunity to collect contributions autonomously up to 500 euros per semester. If one, we say, is studying ten semesters, then it would be 5 000 euros collected over the entire working life per month. [[Abg. Kuntzl:... Tax system!]] In our model, it looks like – now you have to count a bit – that you have a free amount of EUR 1,200 net income and refund 8 percent per month from the amount above until you pay the fees. With a net income of 1 300 euros you would refund 8 euros per month. This is less than many of us pay for their Netflix account, and that should be worth it to us for a better study, for better conditions. The resulting funds would amount to approximately EUR 300 million per year. These are funds that universities can invest for more innovation, better infrastructure and in the future. That is why I would be pleased if we were to have the opportunity to discuss alternative possibilities of financing for our universities in the future. Our proposals are on the table.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-07-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00043_d7e16746	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen! This Scientific Committee has been dealing with a wide range of issues and applications. A very essential topic was a supplement to the COVID-19 high school law, namely to § 109 higher education law. It's about the chain contract regulation. The chain contract scheme has been created to ensure that young scientists do not always have to shirk ad infinitum from one temporary post to another, but eventually come to an indefinite position. That's a good intention. Now, on the occasion of the Covid crisis, it has been found that some who, for example, have such a temporary job as project-related ones, have not been able to carry out their project tasks in time through the Coronalockdown. For these, there is a unique possibility of extension beyond the chain contract scheme under this particular condition. This applies from today's point of view until 30.9.2021. This applies not only to those who sit on project sites, but also to others who, for example, are under a time pressure to qualify, for example, to conclude a qualification agreement under collective agreement. This also applies to this group of persons, so that no one in a positional relationship has a disadvantage due to the braking that Covid has caused. At the same time, we know that this § 109 has been controversial since it existed, for in practice it has not always fulfilled the intention that has been put on it. It may even have happened there and there that someone then does not – hurra! – was allowed to change to an indefinite service relationship, but perhaps even a job was discontinued, so that an automatic approach would not occur. That is why I particularly welcome the fact that the Ministry of Science is working together with the association of universities and the public service trade union and is really seeking a thorough review and reorganisation of this paragraph – a very good undertaking. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Another topic – and I would like to say thank you to Mr Künsberg Sarre of the NEOS – which has been introduced concerns Ghostwriting. It's incredible, these paid plagiars! There are companies that offer on their homepage, how much a dissertation, a Bachelor's thesis or a diploma thesis costs, and ask for fraud. Finally, this request is intended to create a legal situation which, in addition to the previous possibilities, really creates a criminal offence for those who offer it, as well as for those who use it and thus commit fraud in the academic field. It's a welcome undertaking to put one bar in front of the other. There is also the signal from the Committee that, I believe, this will be carried by everyone. Another point was the cooperation agreement of a scientific and technological nature with Brazil. There is also broad agreement. I'm just saying: Cooperation is always good, is always important, but in difficult situations, as is the case at present, it is particularly important. Then there is another request that has been made before. This is about an arbitrary combination of the two art universities in Vienna, i.e. the University of Applied Arts and the Academy of Fine Arts. I would like to add a few points to this. say: The University Law 2002 has brought serious restructuring to all universities and therefore a huge boost to development. We have here in Austria two high-ranking art universities and international reputation. Let's keep working! Thank you.

For example, deaf or deaf people are often assumed to be unable to guide music promotion and thus to pass on children's ability. According to music and movement pedagogues, the processing of music in the brain is a highly creative process – not quite surprising. Thus, American researchers have found that while listening to music, hearing and deaf people, as expected, an area in the brain becomes active, namely, the one responsible for the perception of vibrations. In contrast to the listeners, however, deaf people also have an activity in the so-called auditory cortex. In this case, the processing of acoustic stimuli takes place. This means that the perception of musical vibrations is as real as that of the corresponding tones, since they are processed in the same part of the brain. This comprehensive perception of music is also involved playfully in the work with children and, like inclusion always, brings to everyone something, especially children who can hear because they have a completely different experience of music. For those who can't imagine, there's a great exhibition around the corner. It's called Hands Up. Look at this! You can experience all of this. In addition, we will also try to give you the opportunity that people with learning difficulties can enter into dialogue with you. In this way you can find out which language They need to be able to use or use them so that people with learning difficulties can also You understand. ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-07-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00043_d7e21158	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! Dear Members of the Government! Dear Minister of Education! If it is gratifying that education is at last at the heart of the debate, unfortunately it must be noted that schools and, above all, teachers are increasingly demanding overhuman things: Inclusion, integration, education and care. Some of the most likely major challenges facing our society are now to be trained by schools on their own. I don't think I'm alone wondering how this should work. The proposals put forward by my colleagues are all one step in the right direction, but I am afraid that we are still in a situation where we are trying to cope with a structural problem with mini-steps and a flickwork without introducing a structure. Without a doubt, money is necessary in order not to make the corona crisis, which has many facets, an educational crisis. In addition to the financial resources, we also need something like a general plan of education, which is of major elementary education Up to the universities, an ideology-free analysis of the capacities of our education system provides priorities and invests funds where they are urgently needed. Rules and structures are important, that's clear. But why can't you even go an unorthodox, an unconventional path in the field of education that is as individual as our children? One thing has to be asked rationally: Is our current education system still fit for the future? School holiday support and summer schools, as required today, may only be a first step in a truly future-fitted education system. In an increasingly post-industrial society, education is one of the most important ways to enable our children to have a good future. Every fourth child in Austria is affected by poverty. It is our responsibility that education will not become part of the poverty trap. At a meeting last week, the temporary reduction in VAT was decided, that was beautiful and good, but it is high time to finally relieve the income of Austrians. In the OECD section, Austria is still among the top five, with regard to the burden on individual earners and families. Living, education and education are becoming more expensive from year to year; everything that is the basis of a successful economy is becoming more expensive. I don't think – and I think that we all agree, except once – that our children have no fewer talents or talents than the children in other countries, but an inconspicuous and chronicly underfinanced education system is a explanation why we are now – as is well known – no longer beyond the middle field in the Pisa study. Many of the initiatives envisaged, such as the provision of laptops and tablets, are especially necessary by the still outstanding corona crisis and inevitable in home schooling, but I also fear that new challenges will arise that will be overlooked. It is not only about providing technical infrastructure, but also about responsible use. New technologies are not a cure, but they pose new risks. Already, our children spend most of their time with social media. There are numerous studies to confirm how harmful this development is for teenagers. A prominent example: No less than Steve Jobs has imposed a strict I-Pad ban on his own children because he was aware of the risks. Therefore, we should also be careful not only to create the technical infrastructure for structural use, but also to ensure that our children do not run the risk of being exposed to new risks. The same applies to the really beautiful idea of inclusion, which is basically absolutely welcome, because it is especially good for the souls of the affected children, but also for the families. However, it is also important to ensure that all stakeholders find the necessary sensitive structures needed to ensure optimal inclusion. One presents the parents as well as the children with a huge challenge, and then it does not work, they are left cold. A boy who had to leave an inclusion class in Germany had only one wish, if he had another chance to attend such a class, namely that the second attempt would focus more on his strengths than on his weaknesses – a beautiful approach that we might consider. The training and recognition of special education has been seriously neglected in Austria over the years. There is also a need to catch up on this. Living inclusion does not mean that a good idea cannot be implemented in practice through savings in the wrong place. I would be very pleased if we could jointly find a way without ideological barriers to ensure that an education billion is best used for the benefit of our children and future generations. Children need time, patience and understanding on their school course. All these are things that an exhausted and tired system can no longer give them along. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-07-07-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00043_d7e21361	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Appreciate Seers and Advisors! Schoolbooks are prepared specifically for each subject and school level, taking into account the general and technical curriculum. The curriculum is therefore a basis, an important basis for the development of school books and also a benchmark for their admission. The schoolbooks are applied by the School Book Commission, which is also very precisely regulated. The Declaration of Qualification is an act of the High Authority with a legal basis laid down in § 15 of the School Education Act and must be implemented accordingly. The various stages of the procedure are the following: General Administrative Procedure Act, taking into account the rights of parties and the investigation procedure. In the light of the safeguarding of legitimate interests of third party rights and data protection considerations, publication of procedural steps and documents of an administrative procedure beyond the rights of parties is not provided for in the applicable law. In principle, the application procedure has proved very well in the current form. [[Acceptance with the ÖVP and the Abg. Fischer.]] The Authority has sufficient time, a total of six months, to reach a conclusion with the involvement of many experts and to answer the question of whether the school book is suitable or not. Of course, it is clear that there are misunderstandings here and there, because no application procedure can be perfect. The school book publishing, but also the critical pupils, but especially the teachers as well as the parents, were and are very well able to reveal faulty and inadequate places, and it will respond very quickly. Hamann.]] For this reason, the current approach to the protection of legitimate interests, third-party rights and data protection considerations is balanced and transparent. The Austrian education system works exemplaryly in this respect. By the way, digitalisation control and correction will be much faster. Finally, I would like to wish all students and all teachers a nice and relaxing holiday and thank me for the excellent cooperation.

If a freeman takes the term home into his mouth, he is a nationalist, but if, as in the election campaign, Federal President Van der Bellen advertises with the concept of homeland, it is a positive thing. [[The speaker shows a picture of a poster for Alexander Van der Bellen from the election campaign for the Federal Presidential election 2016 with the letter “Heimat Needs Cohesion”.]] So come on. Please go back to the facts! Let us note that school books must be objective, that terms are not so easily reinterpreted according to the good intentions, depending on the political coloring! And please keep in mind the concerns of our request: In conclusion, we want to know who released the books, how these assessment steps have passed, and that is because we stand for maximum transparency that you, the Greens, otherwise at any time and at any time. Only if it concerns us, you're not right. Democracy doesn't work that way!
